# Interslavic/Panslavic Logbook

This log records the Interslavic/Panslavic lane as a scholarly language-engineering workflow, not merely as another translation. It tracks script policy, terminology, morphology, reviewer-facing uncertainty, and retroactive changes.

## 2026-06-24T06:23:19Z - Archive.org triangulation supplement

- Added an Archive.org supplement to the broader Slavic reference corpus after the user noted that Interslavic/Panslavic term formation should be triangulated against Czech, Polish, and other Slavic mathematical registers, not only Ukrainian/Russian.
- The supplement is deliberately lower-ranked than the existing 20-source university/institute corpus. Archive.org produced useful Polish archival algebra and Czech algebra bundles, but also noisy metadata, access-restricted files, and false positives.
- Current Interslavic policy remains unchanged: use `telo` for noncommutative body/division-ring contexts, `polje` for commutative fields, and keep `kolco` as the project-continuity ring term while documenting `okruh`, `pierscien`, `kolobar`, and `prsten` as Slavic comparison points.
- Future terminology notes should distinguish strong current mathematical witnesses from weak archival/register witnesses.

## Standing Principles

- Treat Interslavic as one language with script variants, not as separate Latin/Cyrillic translations.
- Keep Latin-script Interslavic as the lexical working form for now because the available dictionary/project materials and the first pilot were gathered in Latin orthography.
- Add Cyrillic-script renderings as deterministic reader variants, especially where user expectations or Slavic-facing readership make Cyrillic useful.
- Do not silently normalize away uncertainty. If a term is a coinage, an internationalism, or a compromise, record that fact.
- Prefer broad Slavic intelligibility over imitating any one national standard.
- Keep mathematical internationalisms when they reduce ambiguity: `invariant`, `kovariant`, `kontravariant`, `modul`, `transvekcija`.
- Avoid making Interslavic look like rough Russian, rough Polish, or rough Serbian. The point is pan-Slavic readability.
- Motivate every recurring technical term more rigorously than in Ukrainian/Russian. The motivation must say whether the choice is native/pan-Slavic, international, borrowed, coined, or chosen for script stability.
- If later papers make an Interslavic term look wrong, revise the earlier term openly and rebuild cumulative outputs. This revision trail is part of the research value of the project.

## Script Policy

### Current State

- Paper 01 v001 was produced in Latin script.
- The user expected Cyrillic for Interslavic at first glance, then noted that Latin may also be acceptable because some Slavic languages use Latin.
- Decision: preserve Latin v001 and add Cyrillic variants going forward rather than replacing the Latin text.

### Working Policy

- Source-of-truth text for Interslavic lexical decisions: Latin script.
- Public/readable outputs should include:
  - Latin Interslavic PDF.
  - Cyrillic Interslavic PDF when the paper is ready for pilot/public review.
- The Cyrillic output should be generated by an explicit transliteration table and then visually inspected.
- Any Cyrillic-specific correction must be back-propagated or logged. It must not become an untracked divergent translation.

### Initial Latin-to-Cyrillic Mapping Candidate

This table is a working draft, to be tested against Interslavic reference materials before it becomes final:

| Latin | Cyrillic |
| --- | --- |
| a b v g d e z i j k l m n o p r s t u f h | а б в г д е з и ј к л м н о п р с т у ф х |
| c | ц |
| č | ч |
| ć | ч / ћ pending review |
| š | ш |
| ž | ж |
| ě | є / е pending review |
| y | ы |
| å | о / а pending review |
| ę | е / є pending review |
| ų | у pending review |
| lj/nj | љ/њ optional; default keep лј/нј pending policy |

Notes:
- The Cyrillic table should not be applied blindly to German/Italian/Latin bibliographic titles inside footnotes.
- Math identifiers and TeX commands must be excluded from transliteration.
- Proper names need a separate decision: `Gordan`, `Maisano`, `Pascal`, `Hilbert`, `Noether`, etc.

## Paper 01 v001 Decisions

### General Register

- Chose a direct, modern, pan-Slavic mathematical register.
- Used many international mathematical terms because they are both recognizably Slavic-adapted and mathematically stable.
- Avoided heavy archaism even though historical Interslavic material sometimes permits more archaic choices.

### Key Terms

- `Formensystem` -> `sistem form`
  - Reason: transparent to all Slavic readers; avoids over-coinage.
  - Status: solid for pilot.
- `ternäre biquadratische Form` -> `ternarna bikvadratična forma`; title expanded to `ternarna forma četvrtogo stepena`.
  - Reason: source fidelity plus modern clarity.
  - Status: solid for pilot.
- `relativ vollständiges System` -> `odnosno polny sistem`
  - Reason: literal technical phrase, intelligible in context.
  - Status: review but acceptable.
- `Überschiebung` -> `transvekcija`
  - Reason: classical invariant theory term; matches international/transvectant family.
  - Status: solid for pilot.
- `Faltung` -> `svijanje`
  - Reason: pan-Slavic native equivalent for folding; chosen over a heavier internationalism.
  - Status: needs reviewer check because in invariant theory it may behave closer to contraction.
- `Formenreihe` -> `ręd form`
  - Reason: mirrors Ukrainian/Russian `ряд форм`; definition in text disambiguates.
  - Status: needs reviewer check.
- `Reduzent` -> `reducent`
  - Reason: technical coinage directly corresponding to Noether's historical term.
  - Status: needs reviewer check; definition kept explicit.

### Morphology/Style Choices

- Used `jest/sut` rather than omitting copula.
  - Reason: explicit mathematical predication reads cleaner in formal prose.
- Used `obći` for general/common.
  - Reason: broadly intelligible and already expected in Interslavic contexts.
- Used `konečny`, `poznany`, `reducibilny`.
  - Reason: international/adjectival forms reduce ambiguity in technical statements.
- Used `izslědovanja` for investigations.
  - Reason: transparent across Slavic space, but should be checked against dictionary preference.

### Paper 01 Follow-Up

- Add Cyrillic script variant as a distinct rendered artifact while keeping Latin v001 unchanged.
- Build a small transliteration validator: ignore TeX commands/math, transliterate text nodes only, and report unmapped non-ASCII characters.
- After papers 02--03, revisit whether `svijanje` remains the best term or whether a contraction/transvection-family term is better.
- For cumulative Interslavic output, keep both cumulative Latin source/PDF and cumulative Cyrillic source/PDF once the Cyrillic path is implemented.

## 2026-06-09: Paper 01 Cyrillic Variant Implemented

### Tooling

- Added `tools/interslavic_latin_to_cyrillic.ps1`.
- The tool treats Latin Interslavic as the source-of-truth and generates a Cyrillic reader variant.
- The tool is TeX-aware:
  - Preamble is preserved.
  - TeX commands and structural arguments such as `\begin{...}`, `\end{...}`, and `\vspace{...}` are preserved.
  - Math mode is preserved, except human text inside `\text{...}` is transliterated.
  - `\emph{...}` is preserved as Latin, because Paper 01 uses it for German/Italian bibliographic titles.
- Dictionary route:
  - Exact single-token mappings from `RU kirillica.txt` are preferred when available.
  - Fallback table is used for technical or inflected words not present in the dictionary.

### Current Fallback Rules

- `ě -> е`
- `ę -> я`, except after `č/ć/š/ž`, where observed dictionary patterns give `ча/ша/жа`
- `ų -> у`
- `å -> о`
- `y -> ы`
- `č/ć -> ч`
- `š -> ш`
- `ž -> ж`
- `c -> ц`
- `j -> ј`
- `lj/nj -> ль/нь` in fallback
- Roman labels such as `I`, `II`, `III`, `IV`, and `XVII` are preserved as Latin Roman numerals.
- A small protected-Latin list is used for bibliographic words outside `\emph{...}`: `Sitzungsberichte`, `Math`, `Annalen`, `Giorn`, `Battaglini`, `Rend`, `Circ`, `Mat`, `Palermo`, `Memoria`, `Accademia`, etc.

### Generated Artifacts

- Source: `translations/paper01/interslavic/v001/Noether_Paper01_Interslavic_v001.tex`
- Generated Cyrillic TeX: `translations/paper01/interslavic-cyrillic/v001/Noether_Paper01_Interslavic_Cyrillic_v001.tex`
- Transliteration report: `translations/paper01/interslavic-cyrillic/v001/Noether_Paper01_Interslavic_Cyrillic_v001_transliteration_report.json`
- Rendered PDF: `renders/paper01/Noether_Paper01_Interslavic_Cyrillic_v001.pdf`
- Audit text: `renders/paper01/audit-text/Noether_Paper01_Interslavic_Cyrillic_v001.txt`
- Audit PNGs: `renders/paper01/audit-screenshots/Noether_Paper01_Interslavic_Cyrillic_v001_page-1.png` through page 3.

### Audit Result

- Tectonic rendered the PDF successfully.
- Kept TeX log exists at `translations/paper01/interslavic-cyrillic/v001/Noether_Paper01_Interslavic_Cyrillic_v001.log`.
- Log scan found no missing-character warnings and no overfull boxes.
- Remaining TeX warnings are underfull spacing warnings and the known absolute Windows-font portability warnings.
- Poppler `pdftotext` extraction is readable UTF-8.
- All three rendered PNG pages were visually inspected; no blank pages, clipping, or broken glyph rendering observed.

### Review Flags

- This is a deterministic reader variant, not an independent translation.
- `lj/nj` and nasal-vowel reflexes should be reviewed against Jan/authority feedback before calling the Cyrillic orthography final-public canonical.
- Mixed bibliographic Latin/Cyrillic in footnotes is intentional for now: foreign titles remain Latin, but surrounding Interslavic text is Cyrillic. This can be revised if a stricter citation-script policy is adopted.

## 2026-06-09: Paper 02 Introduction Interslavic Decisions

### New/Extended Terms

- `mod` -> `modulo`.
  - Reason: concise international mathematical preposition; repeated phrases with `odnosno polny sistem` read cleaner than a heavier native paraphrase.
  - Status: provisional.
- `adjungieren` -> `pridavati`.
  - Reason: source action is adding/adjoining moduli, not an adjoint construction; native verb is transparent.
  - Status: provisional.
- `simultanes System` -> `simultanny sistem`.
  - Reason: international adjective is clearer than a newly coined native phrase in a classical invariant-theory context.
  - Status: provisional.
- `rechnerisches Mittel` -> `računno sredstvo`.
  - Reason: keeps the computational sense with a Slavic root rather than importing `komputacijny`.
  - Status: provisional.

### Tooling Correction

- Paper 02 introduction contains a final `align*` block listing introduced symbols.
- The first Cyrillic run showed that the transliterator protected `\[...\]` and `$...$`, but not math environments. This caused symbolic Latin variables inside `align*` to be transliterated into Cyrillic and generated missing-character warnings in TeX math fonts.
- Updated `tools/interslavic_latin_to_cyrillic.ps1` to treat `align`, `align*`, `aligned`, `gather`, `gather*`, `gathered`, `equation`, `equation*`, and `array` as math-protected environments.
- After regeneration, the Cyrillic render has no missing-character warnings and the symbol overview remains mathematically identical to the Latin source.
- Bibliographic `\emph{...}` remains protected as Latin. Emphasized Interslavic prose now uses `\itshape` so it transliterates in Cyrillic output.

## 2026-06-10: Paper 02 §10 Interslavic Decisions

### Translation Unit

- Completed Chapter III §10 in Interslavic Latin and deterministic Cyrillic.
- Latin source: `translations/paper02/interslavic/v001/Noether_Paper02_Section10_Interslavic_v001.tex`.
- Cyrillic source: `translations/paper02/interslavic-cyrillic/v001/Noether_Paper02_Section10_Interslavic_Cyrillic_v001.tex`.
- Rendered PDFs copied to `renders/paper02/`.

### Register Difficulty and Conflict Policy

- Mathematics is relatively programmatic here: recurring frames such as `Redukcije`, `Redukcijne formuly`, `Izvodženje formul`, `jest reducent`, and `po § ...` make Interslavic production more tractable than free literary prose.
- The hard part is canonical stability, not sentence production. The workflow must prevent accidental over-Russian, over-Polish, or over-Czech defaults from becoming silent edition terms.
- Conflict-resolution rule for Interslavic terms: first preserve established project lanes; then prefer transparent shared Slavic or international roots; then record reviewer status when the choice defines semi-constructed mathematical register rather than merely translating a sentence.
- Cross-script policy remains: Latin Interslavic is the editable source-of-truth; Cyrillic Interslavic is a deterministic reader variant unless later authority review requires manual divergence.

### Term Choices

- `Formen fünfter Ordnung` -> `Formy petogo poręda`.
  - Reason: keeps the coefficient-order title pattern from §§7--9. `petogo` follows the regular ordinal `pety/petogo` rather than an uncertain nasalized form.
- `Faltung von K,j,\A mit \nu; f mit H` -> `Svijanje K,j,\A s \nu; f s H`.
  - Reason: stable continuation of the Paper 02 `Faltung` lane; the display remains compact and formula-like.
- `Irreduzible Formen` -> `Nereducibilne formy`.
  - Reason: keeps the international reducibility family paired with `reducent`.
- `Formenreihe` -> `ręd form`.
  - Reason: matches the §7 tabular construction procedure without adding extra algebraic structure.
- `zerfallende Formen` -> `razložime formy`.
  - Reason: concise and pan-Slavically transparent for factor-decomposable forms; reviewer-facing for mathematical register.
- `Die doppelte Berechnung ... gibt Anlaß zur Reduktion` -> `Dvojno izračunanje ... daje povod k redukciji`.
  - Reason: avoids a Germanic syntax calque while preserving the proof role of the double computation; `povod` remains review-needed.
- `Ableitung der Formeln` -> `Izvodženje formul`.
  - Reason: continues the existing `izvod/izvodženje` lane in earlier Paper 02 material; review-needed for morphology and stylistic naturalness.
- `dualistisch entgegengesetzt` -> `dualistično protivopoložny`.
  - Reason: keeps Noether's duality language explicit and readable.
- `als Modul adjungiert` -> `adjungovano kako modul`.
  - Reason: keeps the `adjungovati` family tied to the §7/§9 adjoining-modulus terminology; review-needed.

### Script and Audit Handling

- Deterministic Cyrillic generation was kept on the fallback path for consistency with §§8--9, even though the RU kirillica dictionary is locally available.
- The transliterator was tightened to protect `\mathrm{...}` and `\mbox{...}` arguments. This prevents nested-array labels and prose cross-labels A/B/C/D from becoming Cyrillic letters in the reader variant.
- Visual audit confirmed both Interslavic PDFs render two readable pages; math labels and dense formula blocks are stable.

- Methodological correction: Ukrainian/Russian evidence is valuable but insufficient for Interslavic. Constructed-language responsibility means weighing West Slavic, South Slavic, and Slovenian data explicitly instead of letting the model's East Slavic familiarity dominate by inertia.
- `nekomutativno telo`: remains a strong choice for division-body contexts. Czech `nekomutativni teleso`, Polish `nieprzemienne cialo`, Bulgarian noncommutative-body evidence, and Croatian `tijelo` all support a body/division-ring term distinct from `polje`.
- `polje`: remains reserved for commutative fields and field extensions. This keeps `cislove polja`, `podpolja`, and `razpadno polje` semantically apart from noncommutative `telo`.
- `razpadno polje`: kept for current Paper32 continuity, but Czech `rozkladove teleso` and Polish `cialo rozkladu` make `rozkladno polje` a serious reviewer-candidate. Do not change midstream without a deliberate retroactive glossary pass.
- `jednostranno prosty ideal`: now supported by Czech `leve idealy` and Polish `ideal lewostronny`; the Interslavic form stays left/right-neutral because Noether's German says one-sided, not specifically left.
- `primitivny idempotent`: now supported by Czech exact `primitivni idempotent` and Polish idempotent evidence. This is a safe internationalism, not a place for artificial Slavic coinage.
- `kolco`: remains edition-continuity vocabulary, but the expanded corpus shows why it is not an obvious canonical universal. Czech/Slovak `okruh`, Polish `pierscien`, Slovenian `kolobar`, and South Slavic `prsten` all need to remain visible in the rationale when reviewer feedback arrives.
